# THE SUBSTRATE COURSE
## A Pedagogical Companion to the GUM Program — v3-ext (Tier-6 fold edition; supersedes v2-ext (externally-revised edition), which superseded Internal Review Draft v0.1)

### PART I OF IX

*(Companion to GUM-Ω v2.0.1. Estimated typeset length of the full book: 110–140 pp. Delivery plan: Part I — front matter, Chapter 0 [the history and the wager], Chapter 1 [continuum mechanics toolkit], Chapter 2 [rotations, textures, topology toolkit]. Part II — the action, balance laws, the linear spectrum, photon exactness, the chiral vacuum. Part III — the quantum sector. Part IV — the ħ closure [the course centerpiece]. Part V — electrodynamics and gravitation. Part VI — the knot, the pair law, statistics. Part VII — families, neutrino, color. Part VIII — one world and nucleation. Part IX — experiments, the method, solutions hints, glossary. Each part is delivered on "continue.")*

---

## PREFACE: WHO THIS BOOK IS FOR, AND WHAT IT ACTUALLY TEACHES

This book teaches the GUM program — *Geometrische Umdeutung Mechanik*, the speculative framework in which quantum mechanics, electromagnetism, gravitation, mass, the family structure, and confinement are derived as response modes of a single chiral micropolar solid — at a level readable by a strong graduate student and useful to a postdoc entering the program. It is a companion, not a replacement, for the consolidated paper GUM-Ω v2.0.1: the paper is written to be *audited*; this book is written to be *learned from*. The paper states Theorem IV.G.2 in four lines. This book spends four pages on it, because the four lines compress a two-scale homogenization argument that a student meeting it cold will either absorb wrongly or memorize emptily, and both outcomes are failures of teaching.

Two warnings define the book's character, and you should read both before Chapter 0.

**First: GUM is speculative, and this book will not let you forget it.** Every boxed result in this book carries the paper's ledger class — [DF] derived-form, [DW] derived-with-window, [CAL] calibrated, [IM] import, [CJ] conjecture — and the class is part of the content. When you learn that the photon is exactly massless in GUM, you are learning a theorem about a model ([DF]); when you learn that the charged-lepton mass spacings come out at the O(1)-coefficient level, you are learning a ≈2σ-equivalent structural result with one post-hoc-corrected factor on its record ([DW], and the record is in this book); when you learn that the neutrino is the pitch quantum of the vacuum's chiral order, you are learning a construction with a priced discrete choice (flag F12) and a dated wager attached (stake S1). A student who finishes this book unable to state the epistemic grade of each claim has failed the course even if every derivation is at their fingertips.

**Second: the failures are on the syllabus.** The GUM corpus contains three self-caught blocking defects and a registry of fourteen adjudicated kill conditions, and this book treats them as primary teaching material. Chapter 3 teaches constitutive objectivity *through* the M-1 defect (the version of the theory that accidentally gave the photon a mass, and why restoring frame-indifference cured it). Chapter 11 teaches radiative self-consistency *through* the superradiant rung (the naive spinning particle that would have torn itself apart, and how the theory's own radiation theorem forced the correct answer). Chapter 18 teaches the family sector *through* erratum E-F1 (the factor of 2 that hid inside an error bar for exactly one review cycle). Physics education usually presents finished structures and hides the collapsed scaffolding; this program's scaffolding is unusually well documented, and learning to see *where* theories break — and what an honest repair looks like as opposed to a rescue — is the transferable skill this book most wants to install. We call this failure-forward pedagogy, and it is the reason Part IX's final chapter is about method rather than physics.

**Prerequisites.** Graduate quantum mechanics (through scattering and identical particles); graduate electrodynamics; special relativity at the field-theory level; general relativity at the introductory level (you should know what a connection and a torsion tensor are, or be willing to learn in Chapter 14); classical mechanics through Hamilton–Jacobi; statistical mechanics through the H-theorem. You do *not* need prior exposure to continuum mechanics, soliton theory, stochastic processes, liquid-crystal physics, or the foundations-of-QM literature — Chapters 1, 2, 7, 15, and 6 teach what is needed from scratch, because in our experience these are exactly the subjects a particle-theory-trained reader is missing, and their absence is the single largest barrier to reading the paper.

**Three reading tracks.** *Track G (graduate):* read linearly; do every Exercise marked (★) and (★★); skip (★★★) and the starred sections §n.m\*. *Track A (advanced graduate):* read linearly; do everything; the (★★★) exercises reconstruct pieces of the actual corpus computations. *Track R (researcher/postdoc):* Chapters 0–2 are diagnostic — take the self-test below and skip what you pass; the load-bearing chapters for you are 3–6, 10–12, 17, 18–20, 22, and 24, and the "Audit Notes" at each chapter's end map every pedagogical statement back to its corpus citation so you can verify that nothing has been simplified into falsehood.

**A self-test** (if any item is unfamiliar, the indicated section teaches it): (i) show that ε_ijkε_ilm = δ_jlδ_km − δ_jmδ_kl and use it to expand ∇×(∇×u) [§1.1]; (ii) state why the Cauchy stress tensor of classical elasticity is symmetric, from first principles [§1.3]; (iii) write the SU(2) element for a rotation by θ about n̂ and evaluate it at θ = 2π [§2.1]; (iv) compute δ/δρ(x) of ∫(∇ρ)²/ρ d³y [§7.3]; (v) state Derrick's theorem and why a quartic-in-gradients term evades it [§15.2]; (vi) state Bloch's theorem and the origin of band gaps in one sentence each [§4.4].

**Notation** (matching the corpus exactly; a full table is Appendix N): Latin indices i, j, k are spatial; summation convention throughout; ε_ijk is the Levi-Civita symbol; σ_a are Pauli matrices; tildes mark SU(2) lifts (Q̃, P̃, R̃); 𝔠, κ, g, ê, 𝔦 are the dimensionless closure variables of Chapters 10–12; ⟨r-n⟩ tags numbers from tagged numerical release n; ledger classes appear in square brackets at every boxed claim.

# CHAPTER 0 — THE INVERSION: A HISTORY OF ASKING WHAT MATTER COULD DO

**Learning goals.** After this chapter you can (1) state precisely what Heisenberg's 1925 *Umdeutung* renounced and what GUM's inverse move is; (2) give the two-part reason the mechanical aether program died in the 1890s–1900s and identify which part was a theorem and which a contingency; (3) name the four modern developments that reopened the question; (4) state the GUM wager in its exact, deliberately deflationary form; (5) use the ledger classes as a reading protocol.

## 0.1 What was renounced in 1925

Heisenberg's founding move was a *reinterpretation* — an Umdeutung — of kinematics itself: the position of an electron in an atom was declared not merely unknown but unconstructed, and the algebra of observable transition quantities took over the role that trajectories had played since Newton. Everything distinctive about the quantum formalism — noncommuting operators, the measurement postulates, complex amplitudes on configuration space — flows from accepting that renunciation as fundamental. Every foundational program since has been a negegotiation with it: hidden-variable theories (Bohm 1952) restore the trajectory but inherit a wave function on configuration space whose ontological status is the program's standing embarrassment; Everettian mechanics keeps the formalism whole and pays in worlds; collapse models modify the dynamics and pay in new constants nature has so far declined to exhibit.

GUM's move is the *inverse* of Heisenberg's, and the book's title for it is deliberately literal: instead of asking what formalism describes matter, ask **what matter could possess this formalism as its coarse-grained bookkeeping**. The question sounds naive — it is the question the nineteenth century asked about light and lost — and the entire intellectual content of the program lies in the fact that the twentieth and twenty-first centuries quietly changed the answer's boundary conditions. Understanding exactly *how* they changed is this chapter's job, because a student who thinks GUM is "just the aether again" has not understood the aether's actual failure, and a student who thinks GUM has *evaded* that failure has not understood GUM's actual exposure.

## 0.2 How the mechanical aether really died — a two-part autopsy

The luminiferous aether program (roughly 1820–1905) attempted to realize light as the vibration of an elastic medium, and its most successful artifact is one this course will rehabilitate in Chapter 13: MacCullagh's 1839 medium, whose stored energy depends on the *curl* of displacement alone, W = ½κ|∇×u|², and whose equations of motion are — exactly, not approximately — Maxwell's equations for the transverse sector. Reflection, refraction, polarization: all correct. The program nonetheless died, and the autopsy has two findings that must be kept separate.

**Finding one (a theorem — the real killer of MacCullagh specifically).** No stable classical solid can store energy in rotation alone. We will prove this carefully in §1.2, but the two failure modes can be stated now, and you should hold both: a medium with W = W(∇×u) *assigns energy to rigid rotations* (take u = θ×x with constant θ; then ∇×u = 2θ, so a global rigid turn of the whole medium — which must cost nothing, by the principle of material frame-indifference — costs ½κ·4θ²), and it *assigns zero energy to irrotational shear* (take the symmetric shear u = ½γ(y, x, 0); then ∇×u = 0 identically, so the medium has no rigidity against a genuine shape change). A "solid" that resists being turned but not being sheared is not a pathological solid; it is not a solid at all. Kelvin understood this and spent years building gyrostatic contraptions — lattices of spinning flywheels — to fake a rotational stiffness that ordinary matter's energy function cannot possess. The Cauchy framework of 1820s elasticity, in which a material point carries *position only*, makes the failure inescapable: objectivity forces the stored energy to depend on the symmetric strain alone, and the rotational sector is thereby locked out of the energy budget by theorem. **Hold this finding; Chapter 1 turns it into Proposition 1.1, and Chapter 13's redemption of MacCullagh consists precisely in noticing that the theorem's premise — points carry position only — is a modeling choice, not a law.**

**Finding two (a contingency — the killer of the program at large).** Even where consistent media could be built, the aether behaved *as if it were not there*: Michelson–Morley found no wind; moving matter contracted and moving clocks dilated in exactly the conspiratorial pattern (Lorentz, FitzGerald, Poincaré) that made the frame unobservable; and Einstein's 1905 reorganization showed the entire phenomenology followed from two postulates with no medium mentioned. The aether was not disproven; it was *fired for redundancy*. But — and this is the point the folklore version misses — the firing was for redundancy, not for contradiction, and Bell himself spent pages insisting that the Lorentzian pedagogy (a real medium in which moving instruments are dynamically contracted, so that the frame hides itself) remains perfectly coherent, and becomes *live* again the moment anything in physics needs a preferred frame. Whether anything does is the deepest fork in quantum foundations — it is the fork of Chapter 21 — and GUM's position, stated here so you can watch for it: quantum nonlocality, taken realistically, is exactly such a need, and a medium that hides its frame *dynamically* (Finding two's conspiracy, now derived rather than posited — Chapter 5) while *paying Bell's tax* through a physically instantiated simultaneity structure (Chapter 21) is the Lorentzian fork executed in full.

So the autopsy's summary, which you should be able to reproduce on demand: **MacCullagh died of a theorem whose premise was optional; the program died of a redundancy that quantum nonlocality reopens.** GUM is the claim that both deaths were premature — a claim that obligates it, as we will see in brutal detail, to pass every experiment that killed its ancestors, with margins printed.

## 0.3 The four modern reopenings

Four developments, none of which existed in 1905, are GUM's actual foundations, and each gets a full chapter later; here you need only the shape of each.

**(1) Generalized continua (Cosserat 1909; Eringen 1960s–90s).** The Cosserat brothers, two years before their work vanished into sixty years of obscurity, built the elasticity of media whose points carry an *orientation* — an attached triad — in addition to position. The stress tensor of such a medium is not symmetric; its antisymmetric part is balanced by the divergence of a *couple stress*; and, decisively for us, its energy function may depend on rotational gradients *objectively*, because the rotation in question is the micro-frame's rotation relative to the material, not an absolute one. Finding one's theorem evaporates: MacCullagh's energy is the healthy couple-stress sector of a micropolar solid. Chapter 1 builds this from scratch.

**(2) Mechanical metamaterials (2000s–present).** It is one thing to write a chiral micropolar energy function; it is another to hold a material governed by one. Frenzel, Kadic, and Wegener's 2017 lattice — a 3D-printed structure that *twists when you compress it*, an effect forbidden in any centrosymmetric Cauchy medium — is the laboratory demonstration that the constitutive class GUM postulates for the vacuum is *manufacturable*, with measured moduli, including the chiral transduction coefficient χ that this course will ask to carry the weak interaction's parity violation. §1.4 does the symmetry analysis.

**(3) Emergent relativity and analog gravity (Unruh 1981; Volovik 1990s–2000s).** Sound in a flowing fluid propagates on an effective curved metric; quasiparticles in superfluid ³He-A see emergent gauge fields and an emergent Lorentz cone; and — the result Chapter 14 leans on hardest — a *self-sustained* medium (one whose ground state exists at zero external pressure) has a vacuum energy that gravitates as the *grand potential*, which vanishes in equilibrium by the Gibbs–Duhem identity, without fine-tuning. The 10¹²⁰ vacuum-energy catastrophe, the standard instant-death objection to any structured vacuum, has a known cancellation mechanism *precisely and only* in the medium class GUM inhabits. This does not make GUM right; it makes the instant-death objection wrong, and the difference between those two statements is the kind of distinction this course exists to teach.

**(4) Topological solitons (Skyrme 1961; the modern hopfion/skyrmion laboratory).** A nonlinear field with the right gradient structure supports *knots* — localized, particle-like textures whose stability is topological (a winding number that continuous evolution cannot change) rather than dynamical. Skyrme proposed nucleons as knots of the pion field; the proposal's modern descendants match nuclear physics to remarkable depth; and — the strand that will carry Chapters 18–19 — chiral magnets and liquid crystals now exhibit these objects *on laboratory benches*, including the heliknotons (Hopf solitons stabilized by an ambient helical order) that GUM will cast as neutrinos. Topology is also where fermions come from: Finkelstein and Rubinstein showed in 1968 that a knot can be *quantized as a fermion* if the topology of its configuration space permits a sign, and Chapter 17 proves that GUM's knots permit exactly that sign.

## 0.4 The wager, and how to read this book

Here is the program's claim in the only form it endorses, and you should memorize it because every inflation of it is an error this book will train you to catch: **not** that the world *is* a chiral micropolar solid, **but** that such a solid is the *cheapest known mechanical object* whose response modes include quantum mechanics, light, gravity, mass, families, and confinement — with the price printed on scheduled experiments and the failure conditions signed in advance. The deflationary phrasing is load-bearing. "Cheapest known" is a statement about the current inventory of constructions, falsifiable by exhibiting a cheaper one; "response modes" is a statement about derivations, checkable line by line (that check is this book); "price printed" and "signed in advance" are statements about stakes S1–S7 and the fourteen-kill registry, verifiable against hashed public documents. Nothing in the wager asserts truth. The program's own Stake Letter self-grades its flagship phenomenological result at ≈2σ-equivalent, and Chapter 24 will teach you the blinding-and-escrow machinery behind that grade — machinery you should carry into whatever physics you do next, whether or not GUM survives its adjudicators.

**How the ledger works as a reading protocol.** [DF] means: given the constitutive postulates (flags F1–F13), this follows with no further input — your job as a reader is to verify the derivation. [DW] means: derived at stated order with a stated window or band — your job is to verify the derivation *and* audit the window. [CAL] means: a number from a tagged, protocol-locked computation — your job is to check the protocol, not to re-derive the number. [IM] means: imported at theorem grade from an owner outside the program — your job is to check the import's *premises* hold in GUM (the corpus's worst near-miss, the Weinberg–Witten theorem, is exactly a premises story; Chapter 14). [CJ] means: conjecture, and the program's rules forbid building on it without saying so. **Exercise 0.1 (★).** The abstract of GUM-Ω claims "exact photon masslessness," "Σm_ν ∈ [0.058, 0.11] eV," "the hadronic level is the emergent Skyrme model," and "ΔN_eff ≈ +0.027." Assign each its ledger class before reading further, then check yourself against the paper's results table. (Two of the four are traps.)

# CHAPTER 1 — CONTINUUM MECHANICS FOR THE IMPATIENT FIELD THEORIST

**Learning goals.** (1) Manipulate the deformation gradient, polar decomposition, and linearized strain fluently. (2) State and *prove* material frame-indifference's consequence for Cauchy media, and diagnose MacCullagh's failure in one line. (3) Construct Cosserat kinematics — the strain pair (e_ij, Γ_ij) — by linearization, verify its objectivity by direct computation, and derive the asymmetric-stress torque balance. (4) Perform the parity bookkeeping that admits chiral couplings and forbids them in centrosymmetric media. This chapter is the longest "toolkit" chapter because the paper's Section II compresses a subject most readers have never met; everything here is standard continuum mechanics [IM] presented for field theorists, with GUM-specific commentary in labeled boxes.

## 1.1 Deformation, strain, and the polar decomposition

A body occupies reference positions X; a motion carries them to x(X, t); the displacement is u = x − X. All local information about the deformation sits in the **deformation gradient**

F_ij ≡ ∂x_i/∂X_j = δ_ij + ∂u_i/∂X_j, with det F > 0 (1.1)

expressing that matter does not tear or interpenetrate — a condition that looks like bookkeeping now and becomes, in Chapter 22, the theorem that particles are created only in pairs (Lemma II.1's contrapositive: changing total knot charge requires det F → 0 somewhere). A line element transforms as dx = F dX; a volume element as dV = det F dV₀; squared lengths as |dx|² = dXᵀC dX with C ≡ FᵀF the right Cauchy–Green tensor, which is the object that *measures shape change* — and, crucially, measures *only* shape change: if the motion is a rigid rotation, x = RX with R ∈ SO(3), then F = R and C = RᵀR = 𝟙, and no strain is registered.

**Theorem 1.0 (polar decomposition)** [IM]. Every F with det F > 0 factors uniquely as F = R·U with R ∈ SO(3) (the *material rotation*) and U = Uᵀ > 0 (the stretch), U = √C. *Proof sketch:* C is symmetric positive-definite, so U = √C exists and is unique; set R = FU⁻¹ and verify RᵀR = U⁻¹CU⁻¹ = 𝟙 and det R = det F/det U > 0. ∎ The decomposition will matter enormously: GUM's central constitutive move (flag F10′, Chapter 3) is that the potential energy may see the micro-frame only *through* its relation to this R.

**Worked Example 1.1 (simple shear dissected).** Let u = (γX₂, 0, 0), the canonical simple shear. Then ∇u has the single entry ∂u₁/∂X₂ = γ, so F = 𝟙 + γ ê₁⊗ê₂. Compute C = FᵀF = 𝟙 + γ(ê₁⊗ê₂ + ê₂⊗ê₁) + γ²ê₂⊗ê₂. To first order in γ, the linearized strain is the symmetric part ε_(ij) = ½(∂_iu_j + ∂_ju_i), here ε_(12) = γ/2; the antisymmetric part ½(∂_iu_j − ∂_ju_i) corresponds to a local rotation by ω = ½∇×u = −(γ/2)ê₃. **The moral to carry:** simple shear = symmetric shear + rotation, in equal parts at linear order. Any energy function that responds to the *rotation* part is responding to something a rigid motion could also produce; any that ignores the *symmetric* part has no rigidity. Keep both halves of this sentence; they are about to become the aether's death certificate.

**Exercise 1.1 (★).** Verify ε_ijkε_ilm = δ_jlδ_km − δ_jmδ_kl by counting cases, then show ∇×(∇×u) = ∇(∇·u) − ∇²u. **Exercise 1.2 (★).** For the pure rotation x = R(θê₃)X at finite θ, compute F, C, and the linearized ε_(ij); confirm C = 𝟙 exactly and ε_(ij) = 0 at first order — strain measures are rotation-blind by construction.

## 1.2 Objectivity, and the theorem that killed MacCullagh

**Principle (material frame-indifference / objectivity)** [IM]. The stored energy density W is a property of the material's *state of deformation*, not of the observer or of the body's orientation in space: for every rotation Q ∈ SO(3), W(QF) = W(F). Physically: superposing a rigid rotation on any deformation costs zero energy.

**Proposition 1.1 (the Cauchy no-go; Proposition V.1 of the paper)** [DF]. In a Cauchy continuum — material points carry position only, so W = W(F) — objectivity forces W = Ŵ(C) = Ŵ(U): the energy can depend only on the stretch. Consequently no admissible W depends on the rotational content of the deformation, and a putative medium with W = ½κ|∇×u|² violates objectivity outright. *Proof.* Choose Q = Rᵀ in the objectivity condition: W(F) = W(RᵀF) = W(RᵀRU) = W(U). For the corollary at linearized level: the rigid infinitesimal rotation u = θ×x has ∇×u = 2θ (verify: (∇×(θ×x))_i = ε_ijk∂_j ε_klm θ_l x_m = ε_ijkε_klm θ_l δ_jm = (δ_ilδ_jj−... — do it as **Exercise 1.3 (★)**, answer 2θ_i), so W_MC assigns the rigid motion energy 2κθ² ≠ 0. ∎ And the complementary failure, now provable in a line from Worked Example 1.1: the *irrotational* shear u = ½γ(X₂, X₁, 0) has ∇×u = 0 (check the third component: ∂₁u₂ − ∂₂u₁ = γ/2 − γ/2 = 0), so W_MC assigns genuine shape change zero energy — no shear rigidity.

**Teaching Case 0 (why this matters beyond history).** The paper's one blocking defect — M-1, in which an early version of the theory wrote its topological-sector potential as a function of the *absolute* micro-texture Q̃ rather than the relative texture P̃ — is Proposition 1.1's violation committed in the orientation sector rather than the displacement sector, and its observable consequence was immediate and fatal: the photon acquired a mass. The cure (Chapter 3's flag F10′) is nothing but the restoration of objectivity, and the same restoration *proves* exact masslessness to all orders. The lesson, in the form you should internalize: **objectivity violations are not aesthetic lapses; they are mass terms for Goldstone modes**, and checking frame-indifference is the first audit you run on any constitutive proposal. You will run it yourself in Exercise 3.4.

## 1.3 Cosserat media: giving the points an orientation

Now enlarge the kinematics: each material point carries, besides its position, an orthonormal triad — equivalently a micro-rotation field **Q(x, t) ∈ SO(3)**, with SU(2) lift Q̃ (Chapter 2 handles the lift; for this section Q suffices). The physical picture to hold: the medium is made of extended grains, or oriented molecules, or (GUM's postulate) oriented cells of structure at scale a, and the grain can *turn relative to the neighborhood's average turn*. Two questions define the subject: what are the objective strain measures, and what happens to the stress tensor?

**Constructing the strain measures by linearization.** Write Q ≈ 𝟙 + φ×, i.e., Q_ij ≈ δ_ij − ε_ijkφ_k for small micro-rotation angle φ(x, t), and keep u small too. Two independent objective quantities exist at first order. The first compares the micro-frame to the displacement field around it:

**e_ij ≡ ∂_iu_j − ε_ijkφ_k** — the *relative strain* — (1.2)

whose symmetric part e_(ij) = ε_(ij) is the ordinary strain (φ drops out of the symmetric part — check it) and whose antisymmetric part is e_[ij] = ½(∂_iu_j − ∂_ju_i) − ε_ijkφ_k|_antisym, which repackages as a vector: e_[ij] = −ε_ijkψ_k with

**ψ ≡ φ − ½∇×u** — the *relative rotation*: micro-frame turn minus the local material turn. (1.3)

The second measures how the micro-frame varies in space: the **wryness** Γ_ij ≡ ∂_iφ_j (the linearization of the finite-rotation object ½ε_jkl(Qᵀ∂_iQ)_kl — Exercise 2.4 makes the finite version precise).

**Verification of objectivity — do not skip this computation; it is the whole point.** A global rigid motion turns *everything together*: u → u + θ×x and φ → φ + θ, constant θ. Then: (i) Γ_ij = ∂_iφ_j → ∂_i(φ_j + θ_j) = Γ_ij ✓ invariant. (ii) For e_ij, compute the shift of ∂_iu_j: ∂_i(θ×x)_j = ∂_i(ε_jklθ_kx_l) = ε_jkiθ_k = ε_ijkθ_k (cyclic identity ε_jki = ε_ijk); and the shift of −ε_ijkφ_k is −ε_ijkθ_k; the two cancel identically: e_ij → e_ij ✓. (iii) Hence ψ → (φ+θ) − ½∇×(u+θ×x) = ψ + θ − θ = ψ ✓. **Every Cosserat strain measure is exactly blind to rigid motions — including the rotational ones that killed MacCullagh — while ψ and Γ retain genuine rotational information** (relative turn; turn gradients). The rotational sector has been smuggled back into the energy budget *legally*. An admissible quadratic energy may now contain ½γΓ_[ij]Γ_[ij]-type terms — rotational-gradient stiffness, MacCullagh's κ reborn — alongside full Cauchy rigidity in ε_(ij). This is the paper's "micropolar escape," and you have now proven it rather than been told it.

**The stress tensor loses its symmetry — and why that is a feature.** Recall why Cauchy stress is symmetric: angular-momentum balance on a volume element, with no internal spin and no surface couples, forces ε_ijkT_jk = 0. A Cosserat element, by contrast, carries spin angular momentum density Jφ̇ (J the micro-inertia) and can transmit torques across surfaces via the **couple stress** m_ji (torque per area, conjugate to Γ). Angular-momentum balance now reads, per unit volume,

**J φ̈_i = ∂_j m_ji + ε_ijk T_jk** — (1.4)

derivable by the standard pillbox argument (**Exercise 1.5 (★★)**: write total angular momentum of a region as ∫(x×ρu̇ + Jφ̇)dV, its flux through the boundary as ∮(x×T·n̂ + m·n̂)dA, apply the divergence theorem, subtract x×(linear momentum balance), and (1.4) is what remains). Read (1.4) twice. In the Cauchy limit (J → 0, m → 0) it *degenerates* to ε_ijkT_jk = 0: the classical symmetry of stress is revealed as the special case of a medium too impoverished to carry torque. In a full Cosserat medium, the antisymmetric stress T_[jk] — which the relative rotation ψ sources through the locking energy ~μ_cψ² — is precisely the torque channel by which displacement dynamics drives orientation dynamics and back. GUM's photon (Chapter 4) lives in the locked limit of exactly this coupling.

**Box: the GUM dictionary so far.** Substrate displacement u ↔ the acoustic/constraint sector (B1); micro-rotation φ ↔ the orientational sector whose locked doublet is *light* (B2) and whose relative branch is *matter* (B3); the locking modulus μ_c ↔ (with m_V) the matter gap m̃; the wryness stiffnesses (α, β, γ) ↔ the photon's stiffness and, through their chiral partners, everything in Chapters 18–20. None of this is derived yet — it is a map of where you are going.

## 1.4 Chirality: the parity bookkeeping

Under spatial inversion x → −x: u is a polar vector (u → −u), so e_ij = ∂_iu_j − ε_ijkφ_k... — but wait: φ is a *rotation*, an axial (pseudo)vector: φ → +φ. Track each object: ∂_i → −∂_i; hence ∂_iu_j → +∂_iu_j (two sign flips), ε_ijkφ_k → +ε_ijkφ_k... careful — work it as a table and you find: **e_ij is parity-even (a true tensor); Γ_ij = ∂_iφ_j is parity-odd (a pseudotensor)** (one flip from ∂, none from φ). Therefore any scalar built from an *odd number* of Γ's contracted with e's — the couplings χ₁e_kkΓ_ll, χ₂e_(ij)Γ_(ij), χ₃e_[ij]Γ_[ij] of the GUM action — is a **pseudoscalar**: admissible in the energy only if the medium's point group contains no inversion (nor mirror) — i.e., only in a *chiral* medium, exactly as a sugar solution rotates polarization and its mirror twin rotates it oppositely. These three χ-couplings are compression-twist transductions: squeeze the medium and it twists. Frenzel–Kadic–Wegener's 2017 lattice measured precisely this response in a manufactured chiral micropolar solid — the constitutive class exists. **Box: why GUM wants χ small but nonzero.** Chapter 4 shows the χ-couplings are the *only* channel connecting the light sector to the matter sector (the "transduction"); Chapter 5 shows their radiative effects drive the emergent Lorentz flow; Chapter 6 shows their condensation on the soft sector builds the blue-fog vacuum; Chapters 18–19 show the *same smallness* of χ that makes parity violation weak makes the neutrino light — one dial, four duties, and the parity bookkeeping you just did is the reason the dial exists at all. **Exercise 1.6 (★).** Show that in a centrosymmetric medium all three χ-terms are forbidden, but a term (Γ_(ij))²(e_kk) would be allowed — chirality is about *odd* pseudotensor count, not about Γ appearing at all. **Exercise 1.7 (★★).** The Frenzel lattice twists by angle per axial strain. Using only linearized micropolar elasticity with a χ₂-type coupling, predict the *sign* relation between the twist of the lattice and of its mirror image, and explain why any Cauchy theory predicts zero for both.

**Audit note for Chapter 1.** Everything above is standard [IM] (Eringen's *Microcontinuum Field Theories* is the canonical source; the Cosserat strain conventions match the paper's App. A.2), except Proposition 1.1's packaging and the Teaching-Case framing, which are course-original. The paper's (2.1)–(2.2) are this chapter's (1.2)–(1.3) with the SU(2) lift installed — which is Chapter 2's business, beginning now.

# CHAPTER 2 — ROTATIONS, TEXTURES, AND TOPOLOGY: THE SECOND TOOLKIT

**Learning goals.** (1) Compute fluently in SU(2) as the double cover of SO(3), and explain *physically* why 2π ≠ identity for the lift — the fact that becomes the spinorial clock and the fermionic sign. (2) Differentiate group-valued fields: Maurer–Cartan currents and their flatness identity. (3) Define and compute topological degree; execute the hedgehog computation ∫b = 1 by hand. (4) Hold the small zoo of homotopy groups the program uses — π₃(S³), π₁(SO(3)), π₃(S²), π₄(S³) — each attached to its physical job. (5) Understand line defects (disclinations) and winding, the raw material of charge (Ch. 13) and confinement (Ch. 20).

## 2.1 SU(2), the half-angle, and the belt

Every rotation R(n̂, θ) ∈ SO(3) lifts to two SU(2) elements ±Ũ, where

**Ũ(n̂, θ) = exp(iθ n̂·σ⃗/2) = cos(θ/2) 𝟙 + i sin(θ/2) n̂·σ⃗,** (2.1)

acting on vectors via v·σ⃗ → Ũ(v·σ⃗)Ũ† (verify this reproduces R(n̂,θ)v for n̂ = ê₃ as **Exercise 2.1 (★)** — a five-line Pauli-algebra computation using σ_aσ_b = δ_ab𝟙 + iε_abcσ_c, an identity you should re-derive rather than look up). Evaluate (2.1) at θ = 2π: **Ũ = −𝟙**. A full turn returns the rotation but *negates* the lift; only θ = 4π restores identity. This is the ℤ₂ that runs through the entire program: π₁(SO(3)) = ℤ₂, the double connectivity that the belt trick makes tactile (a belt given a 2π twist cannot be untwisted with its ends held fixed; a 4π twist can — do it with a real belt; the course is serious about this instruction), that Chapter 11 will find in the knot's internal clock (one de Broglie period sends the internal state to *minus* itself — the spinorial clock), and that Chapter 17 will convert into the theorem that GUM's knots are fermions. When the paper writes tildes — Q̃, P̃, R̃ — it is tracking exactly this sign, and the tracking is never decorative.

## 2.2 Differentiating textures: the Maurer–Cartan current

A **texture** is a group-valued field Q̃(x) ∈ SU(2). Its natural derivative is the su(2)-valued current L_i ≡ Q̃⁻¹∂_iQ̃ (left-invariant; the "body-frame angular velocity per unit length"). Two facts to make second nature. *First, the flatness identity:* from ∂_j(Q̃⁻¹) = −Q̃⁻¹(∂_jQ̃)Q̃⁻¹,

∂_jL_i = −L_jL_i + Q̃⁻¹∂_j∂_iQ̃ ⟹ **∂_iL_j − ∂_jL_i + [L_i, L_j] = 0** (2.2)

— the statement that L is a *pure gauge* connection: it has curvature zero because it literally is the gradient of a group element. Every "field strength" in GUM's orientation sector is built to respect (2.2), and the identity is the reason texture monopoles are forbidden (paper's (M2), Chapter 13). *Second, the kinetic form:* Tr[(Q̃⁻¹∂_tQ̃)†(Q̃⁻¹∂_tQ̃)] = ½L_t^aL_t^a-normalized is the unique quadratic invariant — the micro-rotational kinetic energy of the action, and the object Chapter 11 evaluates on the spinning knot to get the inertia integral 𝕀 = (16πJ/3)∫r²sin²f dr (you will do that evaluation yourself, slowly, in §11.2; it is a rite of passage).

## 2.3 Degree: the integer that makes particles

Compactify: a texture with Q̃(x) → 𝟙 as |x| → ∞ is a map from S³ (space plus the point at infinity) to S³ (the group manifold of SU(2)). Such maps fall into homotopy classes labeled by an integer — π₃(S³) = ℤ — the **degree** K: how many times the texture wraps the target. Analytically,

**K = ∫ b d³x, b = −(1/24π²) ε_ijk Tr(L_iL_jL_k),** (2.3)

the pullback of the target's normalized volume form. Degree is the program's particle number: continuous evolution cannot change it (it is an integer varying continuously), *tear-free* evolution cannot change it even at defects (Lemma II.1′, whose proof Chapter 15 walks through: ⟦rev [F-R13]⟧ the material rotation R̃[u] has degree zero because degree is locally constant along any history path keeping det F ≥ δ > 0 — not because the deformation space is contractible, a stronger claim the external audit showed unproven in general ⟦/rev⟧ — so the relative texture P̃ = R̃†Q̃ carries the same K as Q̃ — particle identity guarded by the integrity of matter), and its *conservation-with-exceptions* structure is the pairs-only creation theorem of Chapter 22.

**Worked Example 2.1 (the hedgehog degree — the course's first real computation).** Take the hedgehog ansatz P̃(x) = exp[i f(r) x̂·σ⃗] = cos f + i sin f (x̂·σ⃗), with boundary conditions f(0) = π, f(∞) = 0. Geometrically: at each radius r, the texture maps the sphere of directions x̂ onto the "latitude" f(r) of the target S³; as r runs 0 → ∞, the latitude sweeps pole to pole exactly once — the map wraps the target once, so we expect K = 1. Now verify analytically without grinding the trace: the target S³, parametrized by (f, x̂) with metric latitude f, has volume element sin²f df dΩ and total volume ∫₀^π sin²f df·∫dΩ = (π/2)(4π) = 2π². The hedgehog's pullback of the normalized volume form is therefore b d³x = (1/2π²) sin²f |df| dΩ, and

K = (1/2π²) ∫dΩ ∫ sin²f (−f′) dr = (4π/2π²) ∫₀^π sin²f df = (2/π)(π/2) = **1** ✓. (2.4)

(**Exercise 2.5 (★★★)** asks you to confirm this the hard way, by evaluating Tr(L_iL_jL_k) for the hedgehog directly — the computation that also delivers the sector energies Chapter 15 needs, so the effort amortizes.) Note the structural lesson the paper's Bogomolny sector will exploit ruthlessly: for *any* function 𝒢 of the "latitude" alone, ∫b·𝒢(σ_P) d³x = K·⟨𝒢⟩_{Haar} — topological densities turn spatial integrals into *group averages*, which is how the mass constant C₆ = (64/15π)Λm̃ becomes a Haar quadrature you can do in closed form (§15.3).

## 2.4 The homotopy zoo, each with its job; and line defects

Four groups, four jobs — commit the table to memory: **π₃(S³) = ℤ** (texture degree → particle number, this section); **π₁(SO(3)) = ℤ₂** (double connectivity → the spinorial clock and the possibility of fermionic quantization, §2.1); **π₄(S³) = ℤ₂** (loops *of textures* — the group in which the Finkelstein–Rubinstein sign lives; Chapter 17's Theorem c″ is the statement that this ℤ₂ survives, generator intact, when the configuration space is honestly stratified by the charge defect); **π₃(S²) = ℤ** (the Hopf invariant — linking of preimage circles — the charge of the *collective* sector's heliknoton, i.e., GUM's neutrino, Chapter 19; note carefully that the knot sector, valued in S³, has no such invariant available: the neutrino needs the *vacuum's own order parameter*, valued in S², and that distinction is the entire architecture of Chapter 19). Finally, **line defects**: an order parameter valued in a space M can wind around a line by a nontrivial element of π₁(M); for orientational order the classic case is the disclination, labeled by a Frank angle, and its two GUM incarnations frame the whole particle spectrum — the *frame-convention* disclination whose winding is electric charge (Chapter 13: quantized because π₁(U(1)-convention circle) = ℤ, Coulombic because its far field solves an elliptic equation), and the *network* disclinations of the blue-fog vacuum whose termination requirement is confinement (Chapter 20: a fractionally-wound knot must stay tethered — the flux tube as a topological subpoena). **Exercise 2.6 (★★).** Around a straight disclination line of winding w, the frame angle is α(azimuth) = w·azimuth. Show the gradient energy per unit length diverges logarithmically with system size, and explain in one sentence why this same logarithm reappears as the confining potential's *linearity* when the medium is gapped (hint: Chapter 20; screened vs unscreened Green functions — the answer is the trichotomy Theorem Q-2 in embryo).

**Audit note for Chapter 2.** All mathematics [IM/standard]; the hedgehog quadrature matches paper App. A.1; the homotopy-to-physics table matches Secs. II, VII.F, VII.I–J. The one place this chapter *simplified* is the definition of degree at defects — the honest statement needs the stratified configuration space, and Chapter 17 pays that debt in full rather than hiding it.

# CHAPTER 3 — THE ACTION: THIRTEEN FLAGS AND ONE BLOCKING DEFECT

**Learning goals.** (1) Write the full GUM action from memory and justify every term by symmetry. (2) State each constitutive flag F1–F13 with its motivation *and its price*. (3) Execute Teaching Case M-1: reproduce the objectivity defect, derive its photon-mass symptom, and apply the F10′ cure. (4) Prove Lemma II.1 (charge protection). (5) Perform the dimensional bookkeeping that later chapters lean on.

## 3.1 The inventory of fields and the logic of the build

We have, from Chapters 1–2: a displacement field u (with F = 𝟙 + ∇u, det F > 0), a micro-rotation texture Q̃(x,t) ∈ SU(2), density ρ₀, micro-inertia J. The kinetic energy is fixed by symmetry alone:

**𝒯 = ½ρ₀ u̇·u̇ + ½J Tr[(Q̃⁻¹∂ₜQ̃)†(Q̃⁻¹∂ₜQ̃)]** (3.1)

— translational plus rotational, the second term the unique quadratic invariant of §2.2. (A box worth reading twice: objectivity constrains the *potential*, not the kinetic term. 𝒯 legitimately uses the absolute Q̃, because kinetic energy is measured against the local inertial frame, not against the material. This distinction, glossary-locked in the paper's App. A.5, is what allows the isorotating knot of Chapter 11 to have rotational kinetic energy at all while its potential energy remains rotation-blind.)

The potential energy is assembled in four installments, each answering a physical question:

**W₂ (the quadratic micropolar sector — "what kind of solid?").**
W₂ = ½λe_kk² + μe_(ij)e_(ij) + μ_c e_[ij]e_[ij] + ½αΓ_kk² + ½βΓ_(ij)Γ_(ij) + ½γΓ_[ij]Γ_[ij]. (3.2)
Six moduli: two Lamé constants (λ, μ) for ordinary elasticity; the **locking modulus μ_c**, which penalizes relative rotation ψ (recall e_[ij] = −ε_ijkψ_k, so the μ_c-term is μ_cψ²·2 — the spring that ties micro-frame to material frame); and three wryness stiffnesses (α, β, γ) — rotational-gradient rigidity, MacCullagh's κ reborn legally (Chapter 1's escape). **Flag F1 (locking regime):** μ_c is large — the medium *wants* its frames locked to matter. Price: F1 is a regime choice, not a derivation; its reward is the photon (Chapter 4).

**W_χ (the chiral sector — "which handedness?").**
W_χ = χ₁ e_kkΓ_ll + χ₂ e_(ij)Γ_(ij) + χ₃ e_[ij]Γ_[ij]. (3.3)
The three pseudoscalars of §1.4 — admissible only because **Flag F2** declares the medium chiral (no inversion in the point group). Price: a handedness put in by hand at level zero. Reward: the transduction channel (light↔matter coupling, Chapter 4), the Lorentz flow (Chapter 5), the vacuum condensate (Chapter 6), parity violation and the light neutrino (Chapters 18–19) — the corpus's hardest-working dial.

**W₄ (the Skyrme sector — "why don't knots collapse?").**
W₄ = (κ_S/4) Tr([L_i, L_j][L_i, L_j]). (3.4)
Quartic in gradients, the unique such invariant. **Flag F4.** Its job is Derrick's theorem (§15.2 proves it): under x → λx, gradient-squared energy scales as λ, quartic-gradient energy as λ⁻¹ — a texture with only W₂ shrinks to nothing; W₄ arrests the collapse. Every laboratory soliton in the persistence triptych (FeGe hopfions) survives by exactly this mechanism.

**W₆₊₀ (the topological sector — "what are particles made of?").**
**W₆₊₀ = ½Λ² b_P² + 𝒱_tot(σ_P), 𝒱_tot = m̃²(1−σ_P) + c₂(1−σ_P)², σ_P ≡ ½Tr P̃.** (3.5)
The sextic term ½Λ²b² (b is the topological density (2.3) — six derivatives when unpacked) plus a potential on the group manifold. This pair is chosen for one property that Chapter 15 will exploit without mercy: it admits a **Bogomolny completion** — E₆₊₀ ≥ C₆|K| with saturating first-order equations — making the particle sector exactly solvable at leading order. **Flag F9 (near-BPS):** on knots, the (2+χ+4)-sector energies are small compared to W₆₊₀: ε ≪ 1. Price: a hierarchy assumed. Reward (Chapter 12's surprise): ε is not free — ħ-entrainment *forces* ε_e ≤ 6×10⁻⁴ and cosmology floors it at 1.5×10⁻⁶; the assumed hierarchy became a derived bracket. Window on c₂: positivity and saturability require **c₂ ∈ [−m̃²/2, m̃²/6)** (you will derive this in Exercise 15.4). **Flags F5/F8:** the medium is periodic-on-average at scale a, statistically isotropic — the Floquet structure that gaps the matter branch and the isotropy that keeps low-energy physics rotation-blind.

Notice what (3.5) does *not* say yet: what σ_P is a function *of*. That question is Teaching Case M-1.

## 3.2 Teaching Case M-1: the defect, the symptom, the cure

**The defect (as originally committed).** Write the potential as a function of the absolute texture: 𝒱 = 𝒱(σ_Q), σ_Q = ½Tr Q̃. Innocent-looking — Q̃ is the field, potentials are functions of fields. Now run the audit you learned in §1.2: does a global rigid rotation cost energy? Under u → u + θ×x, Q̃ → g̃Q̃ (the micro-frames turn with everything else), σ_Q → ½Tr(g̃Q̃) ≠ σ_Q in general: **the potential is not frame-indifferent.** Objectivity violated.

**The symptom (derive it — this is Exercise 3.1 (★★), worked here).** What does the violation *do*? Linearize about the ground texture Q̃ = 𝟙: σ_Q ≈ 1 − φ²/2 + ..., wait — carefully: for Q̃ = exp(iφ⃗·σ⃗/2), σ_Q = cos(|φ|/2) ≈ 1 − φ²/8. So 𝒱(σ_Q) ≈ 𝒱(1) + 𝒱′(1)·(−φ²/8): **a mass term for the total micro-rotation φ — including its transverse, co-moving part.** But the transverse co-moving rotation is (Chapter 4) the photon. The absolute-texture potential gaps the photon at the scale m̃ — instantly excluded by m_γ < 10⁻¹⁸ eV, some thirty-plus orders of magnitude of failure. This was the corpus's one blocking defect, and it was caught not by comparing to data but by running the frame-indifference audit — the order of discovery matters pedagogically: *the internal consistency check found what the external check would have.*

⟦rev [Group 4 credit — tier1-spectrum/ADJUDICATION.md]⟧ **Margin note (dated 2026-07, external replication).** The linearized problem was re-solved from scratch by the external campaign, and the case is confirmed — with the symptom sharpened. In the exact linearized spectrum the *gapless branch persists* (it is the translation Goldstone; something always propagates at ω → 0). What dies is the wave's *rotational content*: the surviving gapless branch carries rotational fidelity r = 4μ_c/(4μ_c + m_V²) (verified to 9 digits), while the rotationally-dominant branch is gapped at m̃. Either reading is fatal — a photon that has lost its rotational character is not the photon, so "the light goes dark rather than heavy." **Exercise 3.1, extension (★★).** Diagonalize the coupled (translation, rotation) linearized system and derive r = 4μ_c/(4μ_c + m_V²); show that r → 1 as m_V → 0 and that the M-1 defect sends the rotational weight of the co-moving branch to zero. ⟦/rev⟧

**The cure (Flag F10′).** Objectivity says: the potential may see rotations only *relative to the material frame*. The material frame is R[u] from the polar decomposition (Theorem 1.0), with SU(2) lift R̃[u]. Define the **relative texture**

**P̃(x) ≡ R̃[u(x)]† Q̃(x), σ_P ≡ ½Tr P̃,** (3.6)

and build all of W₆₊₀ (and the finite-rotation locking energy, **Flag F11**: W_lock = 2μ̃_c(1 − σ_P) = μ_cψ² + O(ψ⁴), which merges into 𝒱_tot as m̃² = m_V² + 4μ̃_c) from P̃ alone. Now rerun the audit: under a global rigid motion, Q̃ → g̃Q̃ *and* R̃ → g̃R̃, so P̃ = R̃†g̃†g̃Q̃ = P̃: **invariant, exactly, at finite rotation, to all orders.** The photon-mass symptom is not merely suppressed; it is *symmetry-forbidden* — and that upgrade from "small" to "forbidden" is Theorem II.2 of Chapter 5, exact masslessness. One defect, one audit, one cure, one theorem: hold this sequence as the template for how constitutive physics should fail and recover.

## 3.3 Lemma II.1: particle identity guarded by the integrity of matter

The cure creates an obligation. Topological charge — particle number — was deg Q̃; the potential now sees P̃; are they the same integer?

⟦rev [F-R13, h33 Part A — theory-audit/h33_lemma_II1.md]⟧ **Lemma (II.1′) [DF].** For every deformation *history* along which det F ≥ δ > 0 (u → 0 at infinity), deg R̃[u] = 0 at all times; hence **deg P̃ = deg Q̃ ≡ K**, and K can change only where det F → 0.

*Proof, at teaching pace — and a vindication first.* Earlier printings of this section flagged, honestly: "det F is not linear in u; convexity fails in general," and deferred the general case to the paper's App. F.1. The external audit (F-R13) found that App. F.1 does *not* handle the general case — F.1 *was* the one-line convexity claim itself, now refuted. Your Course was right and the paper was wrong; the flag you were taught to read as a loose end was a vindicated catch. The repaired argument is the one that was always available: (i) For the small-to-moderate-strain class the straight-line homotopy u_s = s·u does connect any admissible u to 0 *through admissible fields* — det(𝟙 + s∇u) > 0 along the path holds exactly for this class, precisely as this section always said, and no further. (ii) For the general case, do not contract the space; follow the *history*: along any physical evolution keeping det F ≥ δ > 0, the map u(t) ↦ R̃[u(t)] is continuous, degree is locally constant along the path, and deg R̃ = 0 at t = 0 (undeformed matter) — so deg R̃ = 0 for as long as matter does not tear. (Contractibility of the full global-embedding class is available only as a deep import from Cerf/Hatcher [IM]; the closure does not need it — the history-path argument carries every physical use.) ⟦/rev⟧ (iii) deg is additive under pointwise SU(2) multiplication of maps in the relevant sense (deg(ÃB̃) = deg Ã + deg B̃ for maps to a group — Exercise 3.3 (★★) proves this via the induced map on H₃), so deg P̃ = −deg R̃ + deg Q̃ = deg Q̃. (iii) deg is additive under pointwise SU(2) multiplication of maps in the relevant sense (deg(ÃB̃) = deg Ã + deg B̃ for maps to a group — Exercise 3.3 (★★) proves this via the induced map on H₃), so deg P̃ = −deg R̃ + deg Q̃ = deg Q̃. (iv) The only escape from the homotopy argument is the failure of its premise: det F → 0 — matter tearing. ∎

Read the contrapositive now, and file it for Chapter 22: **changing total knot number requires tearing matter** — which is why single-particle creation is forbidden and pairs (which conserve total degree and can nucleate through a smooth small-pair intermediate) are the only channel. A lemma installed to make the M-1 cure consistent turns out, nineteen chapters later, to be the pairs-only creation theorem. The corpus calls this pattern "the cure that overpays"; you will see it again.

## 3.4 Dimensional bookkeeping (do not skip)

From (3.5): [b] = L⁻³ (it integrates to a pure number), so [Λ²b²] = E·L⁻³ forces **[Λ] = E^{1/2}L^{3/2}**; [𝒱] = E·L⁻³ with σ_P dimensionless forces **[m̃] = E^{1/2}L^{−3/2}**; from (3.1), **[J] = E·T²·L⁻³**. Three combinations to verify now and use forever: [Λm̃] = E (the knot mass scale, Chapter 15); [Λ/m̃]^{1/3} = L (the knot size R\*); **[Λ√J] = E·T = [ħ]** — the combination that Chapter 10 will reveal as Planck's constant's home. **Exercise 3.5 (★).** Verify all three. **Exercise 3.6 (★★).** Show [a_ψ] = E·L⁻¹ from the B3 dispersion (next chapter's (4.6)) and hence that Λ√(2a_ψ)/c has the dimensions of ħ — the alternate form of the same combination.

**Flags F12 and F13** are introduced here for completeness of the inventory but taught in their home chapters: F12 (Chapter 19) — the ℤ₂ choice of fermionic quantization for the Hopf sector, *consistent but not forced*, the one discrete constitutive choice the neutrino costs; F13 (Chapter 6) — the blue-fog window of the chiral soft sector, 1 < 𝔪 ≲ few. **Audit note.** This chapter's action matches paper (2.2)–(2.4) exactly; the M-1 narrative matches Sec. X's defect registry; ⟦rev [F-R13]⟧ Lemma II.1′'s proof matches App. F.1 *as revised* (v3.0-ext): the original F.1 convexity claim is refuted, this section's honest flag is the catch of record, and the history-path argument above is now the argument of record. ⟦/rev⟧

# CHAPTER 4 — BALANCE LAWS AND THE FOUR BRANCHES

**Learning goals.** (1) Derive both balance laws by variation, tracking the P̃ chain rule. (2) Block-diagonalize the linearized theory completely and derive all four branch families, doing every elimination explicitly. (3) Internalize the sentence that organizes the whole program: *photon and matter are the co-moving and relative projections of one orientation field.* (4) Count modes honestly.

## 4.1 Variation, with the chain rule that matters

Varying u in 𝒜 = ∫(𝒯 − W): the W₂-sector gives the familiar ∂_jT_ji with T_ji = λe_kkδ_ji + 2μe_(ji) + 2μ_ce_[ji] + χ-terms — note the *asymmetric* piece 2μ_ce_[ji], present because e_ij is built from ∂_iu_j *and* φ. But W₆₊₀ also depends on u — through R̃[u] inside P̃. The chain rule: δP̃ = −(δR̃R̃⁻¹)P̃ + R̃†δQ̃|_{δQ̃=0}; the induced force-stress contribution is of order (gradients of the relative sector) and lives in the B1/B4 blocks — this careful bookkeeping (paper App. A.3) is precisely what makes Theorem II.2 an all-orders statement rather than a linearized one. Varying Q̃ (left variation δQ̃ = iη⃗·σ⃗/2 Q̃):

**ρ₀ü_i = ∂_jT_ji; J φ̈_i = ∂_j m_ji + ε_ijk T_jk + τ_i^{(𝒱)},** (4.1)

the second being (1.4) with the potential torque τ^{(𝒱)} = −∂W₆₊₀/∂φ-sector appended. The Cosserat signature stands: **antisymmetric stress is the torque channel** — squeeze-twist transduction (χ), locking torque (μ_c), and topological-sector torque all enter matter's rotational dynamics through it.

## 4.2 Linearization and block structure

Expand about the uniform ground state (u = 0, Q̃ = 𝟙 ⟹ P̃ = 𝟙): fields (u, φ), plane waves e^{i(k·x − ωt)}, k = kẑ. Decompose longitudinal/transverse: u = u_∥ẑ + u_⊥, φ = φ_∥ẑ + φ_⊥, and define the two transverse combinations that diagonalize the physics:

**θ_⊥ ≡ ½(ik×u)_⊥ (the co-moving rotation — the material's own local turn), ψ_⊥ ≡ φ_⊥ − θ_⊥ (the relative rotation).** (4.2)

The quadratic Lagrangian block-diagonalizes into four families. We do each, no steps skipped.

**(B1) Longitudinal acoustic.** ρ₀ω²u_∥ = (λ+2μ)k²u_∥ + χ₁(mixing with φ_∥). At leading order ω = c_Lk, c_L² = (λ+2μ)/ρ₀. **The stiff limit:** as (λ+2μ) → large, this branch's frequency at fixed k diverges — meaning at fixed *frequency* the branch enforces ∇·(response) = source instantaneously: it degenerates into an **elliptic constraint**, ∇²p = −s, pressure as Lagrange multiplier. File this: it is where the quantum potential's *mechanization* lives (Chapter 21), it is silent (transports no energy — Chapter 5's App. E logic), and it is one of *two* elliptic channels (the other, sourced by disclination winding, is electrostatics — the paper's V.B′ separation, taught in Chapter 13; conflating them would make charge proportional to mass, an error this course now inoculates you against).

**(B2±) The locked doublet — light.** The transverse sector has kinetic terms ½ρ_θω²θ_⊥² + ½Jω²φ_⊥² (with ρ_θ the effective inertia of the rotational combination) and potential terms: gradient stiffness for both, plus the locking energy 2μ̃_c-scale·ψ_⊥² *plus* the potential-sector mass — which, post-F10′, attaches to ψ_⊥ **only** (P̃ sees only the relative rotation — this is M-1's cure doing its job in the dispersion relation). Now eliminate the heavy field: at frequencies ω ≪ m̃/√J, ψ_⊥'s equation of motion is algebraic — ψ_⊥ ≈ [coupling·k²/(m̃² + ...)]θ_⊥ — substitute back, and the light combination θ_⊥ inherits a *renormalized* gradient stiffness with **exactly zero mass**:

**ρ_eff ω² θ_⊥ = 𝔎_eff k² θ_⊥ ⟹ ω = ck, c² = 𝔎_eff/ρ_eff — two polarizations, gapless: the photon.** (4.3)

Do the elimination yourself in **Exercise 4.2 (★★)** — it is a two-line Gaussian elimination, and doing it is how you *see* that the masslessness at this (linear) order comes from the potential's blindness to θ_⊥, while the all-orders statement needs Chapter 5's symmetry argument.

**(B3) The relative branch — matter.** The heavy field's own propagating dynamics:

**J ω² ψ = 2a_ψ k² ψ + m̃² ψ ⟹ ω² = ω₀² + c_ψ²k², ω₀ = m̃/√J, c_ψ = √(2a_ψ/J).** (4.4)

Klein–Gordon. This *is* the mass branch: its Floquet dressing (F5) gives band structure at the m̃-scale, its gap quanta are matter, its topological textures are the knots of Part VI, and its band-edge boosted clock is de Broglie's relation (Chapter 15 derives λ = h/p in four lines from (4.4)).

**(B4) Longitudinal twist.** Jω²φ_∥ = (α+β+γ)k²φ_∥ + χ-admixture with B1 — the pump channel connecting compression to twist: the matter↔light transducer whose coupling strength g_χ Chapter 12 will *measure* inside the theory (the X-T1 hybrid-mode computation) at 0.42 ± 0.06.

**Mode count** (the honest audit): 3 (u) + 3 (φ) = 1 (B1) + 2 (B2) + 2 (B3-transverse) + 1 (B4/B3-longitudinal mixing) — six, with the B2/B3 split being a *basis change* (θ vs ψ) of the transverse four, not new content. No mode missing, none doubled.

**The organizing sentence, earned.** The transverse orientation field has two projections: the part that *co-moves* with matter (θ_⊥ — gapless, protected, light) and the part that moves *relative* to matter (ψ_⊥ — gapped, massive, matter). **Photon and matter are two projections of one field.** Every deep structural fact downstream — the transduction channel, the pair-creation threshold's location, the confinement dichotomy (gapless mediator ⟹ unconfined charge; gapped mediator ⟹ confined color, Chapter 20) — is this sentence wearing different clothes. **Exercise 4.4 (★).** A colleague objects: "you got a massless photon by *choosing* the potential to depend on ψ only." Write the three-sentence reply distinguishing a choice from a symmetry requirement, citing §3.2. **Exercise 4.5 (★★★).** Restore the χ-couplings in the transverse block and show they mix B2 and B3 *off-shell* while leaving B2's gaplessness intact — the calculation that previews both the C1 flow (Chapter 5) and Proposition Ω-2's parity protection.

# CHAPTER 5 — THE PHOTON'S FOUR THEOREMS, AND EMERGENT LORENTZ SYMMETRY

**Learning goals.** (1) Prove exact masslessness (Theorem II.2) and understand why it is an all-orders symmetry statement. (2) Prove tree-level achirality (II.3) and its all-energy parity extension (Ω-2). (3) Prove kinematic photon stability (Ω-1) and see why cone-universality is its bodyguard. (4) Derive the C1 velocity flow and run both laboratory controls (Rb: no convergence; graphene: convergence) as worked problems. (5) Understand the "one-shield" architecture: how a medium hides its frame dynamically.

## 5.1 Theorem II.2 (exact masslessness) — the symmetry statement

**Theorem** [DF]. Under uniform co-rotation — Q̃ → g̃Q̃ with the material frame rotated identically, R̃ → g̃R̃ — the relative texture P̃ is invariant; hence the entire potential sector W₆₊₀ + W_lock contributes zero mass to the co-moving doublet **to all orders in fluctuations**.

*Proof, and what it means.* Invariance of P̃ is one line (§3.2's computation). The consequence is Goldstone's logic run in reverse: a *continuous symmetry of the potential* (uniform co-rotation, a three-parameter family) acting nontrivially on the fields guarantees flat directions — the co-moving rotations — protected against mass generation by *any* interaction built from P̃, at any loop order, because the symmetry survives quantization of a potential that never sees the symmetry's coordinate. Contrast with (4.3)'s linearized elimination: that showed masslessness at quadratic order; this shows it is not an accident of truncation. The pedagogical punchline: **M-1's cure did not fix a bug; it installed a symmetry**, and "the photon is massless because the vacuum's energy cannot depend on a uniform co-rotation" is GUM's answer to a question the Standard Model answers by fiat (unbroken U(1)).

## 5.2 Theorems II.3 and Ω-2 (achirality, at tree level and at all energies)

**II.3 (tree)** [DF]. In the locking regime, on-shell e_[ij] → 0 for the gapless dynamics (the relative rotation is frozen out at low ω — that was the elimination of §4.2), which *quenches χ₃* (the only chiral coupling that could split B2's helicities: it is the one contracting the antisymmetric sectors); χ₁ feeds only the longitudinal/constraint block; χ₂'s symmetric contraction enters B2 helicity-blind. Hence zero tree-level circular birefringence; regeneration is suppressed by (ka)². ∎

**Ω-2 (all energies)** [DF] — and the seam that found it. A referee's worry: at ω ≫ ω₀ the eliminated ψ-sector is no longer heavy; do the induced B2 operators grow as (ω/ω₀)² and resurrect birefringence exactly where GRB bounds are strongest? The rescue is *parity bookkeeping* (§1.4): every χ-coupling is parity-odd; integrating out B3 generates B2 operators at **even powers of χ** — parity-even, hence **helicity-symmetric**: whatever grows with energy is dispersion common to both circular polarizations, never a splitting. Helicity-odd effects first arise at O(χ³) with an additional (ka). ∎ *Teaching point:* this proposition was discovered by an audit (AUD-7) stress-testing a consolidation seam — the theorem existed in the theory before anyone in the program had written it down, and the audit's pressure extracted it. Theorems can be found by attempted refutation of one's own text; that is what "seam-checking" means.

## 5.3 Proposition Ω-1 (kinematic stability) — and its dependency

**Ω-1** [DF]. On a common cone (c_ψ = c), the B2 line ω = ck and the B3 hyperbola ω = √(ω₀² + c²k²) never intersect at k > 0; and a lightlike four-momentum cannot equal the sum of two timelike ones with total rest energy 2ω₀ > 0. Hence **no on-shell single-photon → matter conversion and no vacuum photon decay at any energy**: pair creation requires a spectator supplying recoil (Chapter 22's threshold). *Proof of the pair statement (do it — Exercise 5.2 (★)):* in the pair's center-of-momentum frame the photon would need ω = 2√(ω₀² + c²q²) ≥ 2ω₀ with **k = 0** — but a photon with k = 0 has ω = 0. Contradiction. ∎ **The dependency, printed in bold because the paper does:** the proof used c_ψ = c. Cone universality is not merely the Lorentz shield's ingredient; it is **the photon's bodyguard** — if the cones split at high energy, ultra-high-energy photons decay in vacuum, and the observed propagation of TeV–PeV photons across cosmological distances becomes a GUM constraint. Which is exactly why the next section is not optional physics.

## 5.4 The C1 flow: how one cone emerges, and the two controls

Tree level: locking merges the gapless transverse stiffness (that was (4.3): 𝔎_eff is a *combination* of shear and wryness moduli — one cone by construction *if* the relative-sector normalization tracks it, which is F1's content). Radiative level: the χ-transduction is the only light↔matter channel, and dressing by it drives the fractional cone splitting δ ≡ (c_ψ − c)/c̄ according to

**dδ/dℓ = −(g²/c̄²) δ + O(δ²) ⟹ δ(E) = δ_UV / [1 + (g²/c̄²) ln(Λ_UV/E)]** [DW]. (5.1)

*How to read this like a field theorist:* velocity splitting is an **irrelevant operator** in the RG sense — not forbidden, but dying logarithmically toward the infrared. The mechanism (each sector's propagation dressed by loops of the *other* sector, dragging both velocities toward their weighted mean) is generic; what is GUM-specific is the claim that g² = O(1) (strong universal transduction). And *that* claim is what the controls test:

**Control 1 — ⁸⁷Rb spinor condensate (the negative control, worked).** A two-component BEC has two sound cones: density sound c_d and spin sound c_s, with c_s/c_d = √(c₂-coupling/c₀-coupling) ≈ 1/√(190)... measured ratio ≈ 1/14 — a *fourteen-fold* cone splitting. Does it converge? The inter-sector coupling is c₂/c₀ ≈ 0.005 — weak. Available RG running: ℓ = ln(cloud size/healing length) ≈ 5–8 e-folds. Predicted fractional convergence: Δδ/δ ≈ g²ℓ ≈ 0.005 × 6 ≈ **3%**. Observed: the splitting persists, full strength. ✓ **The mechanism predicts its own failure here** — and a mechanism that *cannot* fail is numerology. **Control 2 — graphene (the positive control).** Dirac quasiparticles at v_F ≈ c/300 with effective fine structure α_eff = e²/(ħv_F) ≈ 2 — *strong* coupling to the (already-relativistic) photon sector. Prediction: O(1) logarithmic flow of v_F upward over accessible scales. Observed (suspended graphene, Dirac-point spectroscopy): v_F renormalizes by factor ~1.5–3 exactly logarithmically. ✓ **Exercise 5.4 (★★).** GUM's own case: g² = O(1), ℓ = ln(a⁻¹/lab scale) ≈ 50+. Show δ(lab) ≤ δ_UV/50 and, using the UHECR bound δ_UV ≲ 10⁻¹⁹ (Chapter 14 derives it), that every laboratory Lorentz test is passed with absurd margin — then articulate why the *log-running shape* δ(E) is nonetheless a positive prediction no fundamentally-Lorentz theory shares.

**The one-shield architecture (closing box).** The frame is not hidden by conspiracy; it is hidden by *dynamics plus equilibrium*: one medium in its ground regime gives (i) a common cone (this section) — so kinematics can't see the frame; (ii) Lorentz-contracted moving matter (bound states relax to their boosted shapes — Bell's pedagogy, derivable here); (iii) Born-equilibrium statistics (Chapter 8) — so quantum correlations can't signal frame position either. Three consequences, one hypothesis. The places the frame *can* peek out — non-equilibrium statistics, non-POVM arrival observables, the sidereal probe — are exactly the program's experimental perimeter (Chapter 23).

# CHAPTER 6 — THE CHIRAL VACUUM: A CONDENSATE, MISPLACED AND FOUND

**Learning goals.** (1) Derive the Dzyaloshinskii condensation criterion from scratch. (2) Execute Teaching Case E-H1: why the helix cannot live where three chapters of results assumed it did, and how an audit relocated it. (3) Understand the soft sector (SDiff flatness, ε-lifting) as the corpus's triple-duty manifold. (4) Prove the ε-blindness of the condensation margin (Theorem H2-1) and understand why that, not the O(1) window landing, is the real result. (5) Meet the blue fog: double-twist frustration, the disclination web, statistical isotropy, and the phason.

## 6.1 The Dzyaloshinskii criterion, from scratch

Let a sector of the theory have twist stiffness γ_s, chiral gain χ_s (the linear-in-gradient, parity-odd term that *rewards* twisting — §1.4's χ-couplings projected onto this sector), and a gap Δ_s (the energetic cost of tilting into the textured configuration at all). Ansatz: a conical texture, tilt angle θ, pitch wavevector q along ẑ. Energy density:

f(θ, q) = sin²θ ( −χ_s q + ½γ_s q² ) + ½Δ_s²θ². (6.1)

⟦rev [E2 — gates G24–G26]⟧ **Order qualifier (added in this edition).** The gap term ½Δ_s²θ² in (6.1) is the *quadratic-order* form; the natural all-orders completion consistent with the sector's symmetry is −Δ_s² ln cos θ, which agrees with ½Δ_s²θ² through O(θ²) and therefore leaves the condensation criterion (6.2) — a statement about the θ → 0 instability — *exactly unchanged*. It does move the finite tilt: the literal all-orders reading gives sin θ_c = 0.7924 where the quadratic reading gives 0.688 — a one-line check worth doing (Exercise 6.2′ (★): minimize both forms at 𝔪 = 1.9 and compare). The external replication campaign implemented *both* readings and found the identical threshold, as the derivation predicts; the printed residue values below are the quadratic-order convention and are unchanged. ⟦/rev⟧

Minimize over q first: q\* = χ_s/γ_s, and the parenthesis at q\* equals −χ_s²/2γ_s — the *chiral energy gain*. Small-θ expansion: f ≈ ½θ²(Δ_s² − χ_s²/γ_s). Condensation — the uniform state going unstable to spontaneous twist — occurs iff the coefficient is negative:

**χ_s² > γ_s Δ_s² (the Dzyaloshinskii criterion), with pitch q\* = χ_s/γ_s and margin 𝔪 ≡ χ_s²/(γ_sΔ_s²).** (6.2)

This is textbook cholesteric physics [IM] — and now a loaded weapon, because GUM's family sector and neutrino sector (Chapters 18–19, whose results were derived *first* in the corpus's actual history) both presuppose a condensed helix. Where does (6.2) hold?

## 6.2 Teaching Case E-H1: three chapters on a floor that wasn't there

**The defect.** The corpus's family generator was built on "the vacuum's helical order" with the tacit sector assignment: the helix lives in the relative (B3) sector — the natural guess, since that is where orientation dynamics is richest. Run (6.2) there: Δ_s = m̃ — the locking gap, the *largest* scale in the orientation sector — while χ is small (it must be: it carries parity violation, Chapter 18). The criterion fails by many orders. **The helix, as sited, could never have condensed.** Three review cycles of results stood on it before the audit commissioned to check the foundation (WS1-H) ran the criterion and issued erratum E-H1 — the corpus's longest defect lifetime, printed in the paper's Sec. X with its duration, because the *duration* is the lesson: a wrong tacit assumption survives exactly as long as nobody computes its consistency condition. The audit that caught it was not clever; it was merely *scheduled*.

**The relocation (Theorem H-2)** [DF-structural]. Is there any sector soft enough? Requirement: Δ_soft small enough that weak χ passes (6.2). The corpus contains exactly one candidate, and you already own it from §3.1's inventory: the **SDiff-flat directions of the (6+0) vacuum**. At ε = 0, volume-preserving diffeomorphisms cost the topological sector *nothing* (Chapter 15 proves it: b transforms as a density, 𝒱(σ_P∘ψ) transports — E₆₊₀[P̃∘ψ] = E₆₊₀[P̃]); the (2+χ+4)-sector lifts this flatness at O(ε): **Δ²_soft = O(ε)·(fundamental scale)²**. On the soft manifold, (6.2) is satisfiable for arbitrarily weak χ. The helix lives there — and "there" is a manifold already carrying two other jobs: it is where masses are additive (the SDiff degeneracy of Chapter 15) and where the dark-energy slow mode lives (Chapter 14). One flat manifold, three duties — the cross-lock X-Λν (any *numerical* meV-neighborhood kinship between dark energy and neutrino mass) is flagged [CJ-watch] and, per the charter, refused as numerology until computed.

## 6.3 Theorem H2-1: the ε-blind margin

Now the audit-grade question: the relocated criterion's margin 𝔪 = χ²_soft/(γ_softΔ²_soft) — does it depend on ε, and hence on species, and hence threaten to make the vacuum's phase structure drift with the spectrum? Count the ε's: every ingredient is a *projection onto the soft manifold* of a fundamental coupling, and each projection carries exactly one power of ε (the lifting scale): χ_soft = εχ̄, γ_soft = εγ̄, Δ²_soft = εΔ̄². Therefore

**𝔪 = (εχ̄)²/(εγ̄ · εΔ̄²) = χ̄²/(γ̄Δ̄²) — ε cancels exactly: the margin is a pure number of fundamental couplings** [DF]. (6.3)

⟨r9⟩ evaluates the three projections on the deep-BPS background: **𝔪 = 1.9 ± 0.4.** Now the pedagogically crucial grading, verbatim from the corpus because it is a model of self-honest language: *an O(1) ratio landing in (1, 3) is unremarkable-prior; the ε-blindness is the real result* — the window is structural, incapable of drifting with species, and that structural fact (not the lucky-looking 1.9) is what the family and neutrino sectors are entitled to stand on. Learn to make this distinction in your own work: a number landing well is weak evidence; a number *provably unable to land elsewhere as parameters vary* is a theorem.

## 6.4 The blue fog: frustration, the web, isotropy, and the phason

One crisis remains, and it is observational: a single-axis helix at pitch q — a µm-scale *oriented* vacuum (Chapter 19 will fix q⁻¹ ∼ 4 µm from the neutrino mass) — violates Hughes–Drever-class isotropy bounds catastrophically. The resolution is imported from the one laboratory system that faced the identical problem: highly chiral liquid crystals. **Double twist beats single twist locally** — derive it as **Exercise 6.3 (★★)**: near the axis of a double-twist cylinder the director gains chiral energy along *two* transverse directions, yielding ≈2× the single helix's gain per unit volume — **but double twist cannot tile 3-space** (a frame field cannot be doubly-twisted everywhere; the obstruction is topological, the same reason a hairy ball can't be combed), so the globally consistent state is a **network**: double-twist regions stitched by a web of disclination lines with tension T ∼ γ_soft ln(1/qa_core) and density ∼ q². For near-threshold margin (1 < 𝔪 ≲ few — **Flag F13**, satisfied by (6.3)'s 1.9 ± 0.4), the amorphous network — the *blue fog*, BPIII's analog — beats the single-axis cholesteric: **locally helical, globally statistically isotropic.** F8 is preserved; the Hughes–Drever crisis (the corpus's flag F-hel) is discharged conditional on F13; the family generator and the heliknoton keep their helix, now as *local* pitch.

Three residues, each a chapter-19/20 load-bearer, introduced here so you meet them unburdened: (i) the condensate tilt sinθ_c = √(1 − 1/𝔪) = 0.69 ± 0.11 ⟦rev [E2]⟧ (quadratic-order convention; see the §6.1 order qualifier — externally replicated, gates G24–G26, both readings implemented with identical threshold) ⟦/rev⟧ and the **phason** — the gapless Goldstone of the condensate's phase, with decay constant **f = sinθ_c·√ε·𝔪_Sk ∈ [4, 29] MeV** across the operative ε-range — the mediator whose free-streaming bound (f ≥ 1.4 MeV) becomes the *cosmological floor* on ε (Chapter 19); (ii) the web's naive energy density, ∼10²²ρ_Λ — a heart-stopping overclosure until you recall Chapter 14's Gibbs–Duhem clause: *the network is the ground state; only departures gravitate* (the corpus's exhibit F-H2-1: a mechanism built for one catastrophe quietly defusing another); (iii) the network's disclination web itself — which Chapter 20 will reveal as the confinement infrastructure: a knot with *fractional* frame winding cannot be screened by smooth fields and must stay tethered to the web's lines. The vacuum you have just built is not scenery; it is the origin of families, the neutrino's body, and the quark's prison.
